Let's examine previous algorithm and why it got 1.517. It used optimize_v with adagrad-like on softmax. It chose tau i.e. soft max weight on lags. It also applied top_k boost. We can improve hyperparameters:
- Use exact max active set with p = exp((c - cmax)/tau) normalized; tau should decrease over time to ~1e-3 of bmax to focus on the maximum lags. Or p = normalized top K with weights. Objective is max, at optimum all binding lags maybe multiple; using too few could destabilize. Could use p_k ∝ exp((c_k - cmax)/τ) plus uniform small floor to keep all constraints.
- Use L-BFGS on smooth parameterization? Could be faster for high dimensions. We can use scipy.optimize.minimize with L-BFGS-B on log variables, objective logsumexp, finite gradient vector. But n up to 4096, L-BFGS memory maybe OK. However scipy minimization with constraint sum? We can optimize v on R^n, objective scale invariant unbounded? logsumexp homogeneous degree2; not coercive along constant shifts of v (scale invariance). Need fix sum by normalizing a after gradient? L-BFGS not handle normalization. We can use reparameterization a_i = exp(v_i)/∑exp(v), v constrained e.g. sum v=0 or fix v_n=0. L-BFGS can handle with v without normalization derivative? We can define parameter q (n-1 dimensions) with v_n= -sum first n-1? Complexity. Or use Adam simpler.

```
def _corr_from_p_a(p,a,N):
    P=np.fft.fft(p,N)
    A=np.fft.fft(a,N)
    return np.fft.ifft(P*np.conj(A)).real
```

```
def spectral_project(target, a_start, n, N, iters, blend_start=0.6, blend_end=0.2):
   T = fft(target, N)
   T[abs(T)<eps]=eps
   a = a_start.copy(nonneg, normalize)
   F = fft(a,N)
   best... for it:
      # Newton correction: Fnew=0.5*(F + T/(F+1e-12*max))
      # or phase: Fnew = sqrt(T) sign nearest F
      option random.
      anew = ifft(Fnew).real
      pos = max(anew[:n],0)
      # also maybe mirror negative? Negative parts can indicate phase; use absolute? Maybe `pos = max(anew,0)+softneg`? We can use softplus? But entries with small negative from noise; abs could improve if phase ambiguous. Try pos = np.abs(anew[:n])? For square root sign, if phase wrong, flipping sign makes positive? Since a should be real nonnegative, taking abs may help but can introduce alias? Let's include option variant: pos1=positive, pos2=abs. Score both, pick better. Abs might produce good because if factor is skewed with phase near 0 or π? But a should not have alternating signs. Could be beneficial.
      pos sum; normalize.
      blend = lerp(blend_start, blend_end, progress)
      a_new=(1-blend)*a+blend*pos
      F=fft(a_new,N)
      score.
   return best
```
But taking abs may break real square property. But for initialization only.

We can make a loop:
```
def target_from_sequence(a, lambda_frac, avg):
   c=conv(a); csum=c.sum(); c/=csum
   lam=lambda_frac*(1/(2n-1))
   cap=np.minimum(c, lam)
   deficit=1-cap.sum()
   room=lam-cap
   if room_sum>deficit+tol:
       target=cap+deficit*room/room_sum
   else: target=cap+deficit/L
   floor if any <0? target nonneg.
   return target
```

```
def poly_sqrt_target(target, n):
   L=2n-1
```

Can we improve by using exact polynomial square root of a clipped target in an alternating loop with local optimization? This could be the main algorithm:
1. Start a from seed.
2. Compute c. Choose λ.
3. Create target T = rebalance(min(c,λ)).
4. P = poly_sqrt(T) (choose orientation).
5. Update a = blend(P_pos, old) (or P_pos if better).
6. Repeat with decreasing λ.
Because P is a polynomial whose square exactly matches target's first n lags; by lowering λ, it attempts to make all lags low. This is a deterministic global-like method.

Let's test with small n. n=2: start uniform a=(0.5,0.5), c=(0.25,0.5,0.25), avg=1/3=0.333, target λ maybe 0.35. cap=(0.25,0.35,0.25), sum=0.85, deficit=0.15, room=(0,0,0). cap all at λ? room for lags0,2=0.1 each sum0.2, add 0.075 each => target=(0.325,0.35,0.325). Let's analyze fixed point: If c has max≤λ, cap =c, target=c, recurrence returns a, no change. If c has peaks>λ, cap reduces peaks and redistributes to lower lags. The root polynomial might have a with lower max? Likely.

We can make a strong algorithm around this:
- Multi-start with sequences.
- For each sequence, perform a "capping iterations" loop:
  - current best score.
  - λ_schedule: start at current max * factor (0.95), lower gradually to target 1.1*avg? But if too low may cause numerical/negative; if no improvement, increase.
  - At each lambda: create target, poly_sqrt, positive/abs variants, update a; maybe blend with old to maintain positivity. Run small local optimizer after to polish.
- Since recurrence is deterministic, it may converge to local minima. Different starts and λ help.

Let's implement `cap_square_iterate(a, n, max_iters, lambda_frac_list, blend, deadline, rng)` maybe.
Pseudo:
```
def cap_square_step(a, lambda_frac, n):
   c=conv_fft(a); c/=sum
   avg=1/(2n-1)
   lam=lambda_frac*avg
   if lam < 1.01*avg: lam=1.01*avg
   ccap=min(c,lam)
   deficit=1-ccap.sum()
   room=lam-ccap
   if room.sum()>deficit:
       target=ccap + deficit*room/room.sum()
   else:
       target=ccap + deficit/L
   target = max(target, 1e-15)
   # ensure endpoint for orientation
   eps_end = 1e-12? target[0]=max(target[0], 1e-12); target[-1]=...
   p_imp = poly_sqrt_target(target, n)
   candidates = [proj(p_imp), proj(abs(p_imp)), proj(max(p_imp,0))]
   for cand: score; if improved maybe return cand, else keep best maybe blend old.
   return best (old if none improved)
```

Let's optimize poly_sqrt recurrence:
```
def poly_sqrt_target(target, n, side=None):
    L = 2*n - 1
    t = np.asarray(target, float).copy()
    if len(t)<L: pad
    t = t[:L]
    # choose side if None: if t[0] >= t[L-1] forward else backward
    if use backward:
       tr = t[::-1]
       q = recurrence(tr, n)
       p = q[::-1]
    else: p=recurrence(t,n)
    return p
```
Recurrence details with dot: For k in range(1,n): lower = 0; if k>1: lower = np.dot(q[1:k], q[k-1:0:-1]). But for large k, slicing creates view? q[1:k] and reversed. This dot O(k). Over k total n^2/2. For n=8192 ~33M, maybe 0.2s per call. Need not too many. We can restrict cap iterations for large n to 2048. Or implement an FFT-based square root to be faster.

FFT Newton for square root of polynomial (first n coefficients): Given C series (length n), want P series length n such that P^2=C truncated. We can do iterative Newton with doubling:
- Start with p0 = sqrt(C[0]).
- For m lengths 1,2,4,... up to n: solve P (length 2m) such that P^2 ≡ C mod z^{2m}. Given current P_m ≈ C^{1/2} mod z^m. Newton: P_new = (P + C * (1/P)) / 2 truncated. Using convolution. Since P has nonzero constant, 1/P can be computed from known P_m? Simpler recursive formula can be computed using convolution but O(n log n) with FFT. We can use existing numpy FFT to invert series.
Algorithm for series I = 1/P modulo z^m known? We can maintain R=1/P. New P = 0.5*(P + C*R) truncated 2m; new R = 2*R - P_new*R mod 2m. This uses convolutions via FFT. But implementing truncation and padding must be careful. n up to 8192, FFT length 2n. This will be faster than O(n^2) but more code. However recurrence O(n^2) maybe acceptable if cap steps limited. But for n=8192 and many iterations, maybe too slow. We can implement for n<=4096 with recurrence, for >4096 use Fourier sqrt/Newton. Simpler: use recurrence for n<=2048, and interpolate from 2048 for larger. We can avoid cap step at 8192; just local optimize at large from interpolated best. That's good.

Previous final length 2048; cap step can heavily optimize 2048. For 4096, we can interpolate best 2048 and local optimize; maybe also a few cap steps if time and n=4096 (recurrence 16M, maybe ok for few). We'll make cap step limited for >2048: fewer iters.

Could improve recurrence with `np.convolve`? For each k, lower exactly coefficient of q*q at k minus 2q0 qk. But we can precompute? There is a way: q_k = (c_k - 0.5*(conv(q,q)_k - q_k^2?))? Not solving. Maybe use Newton.

For k large, c_k~ (n-k?)/n^2 ~0.0005, lower~? Could involve cancellation but relative OK? Coefficients ~1e-3, p0 5e-4, product 2p0 p_k ~5e-7? Wait c_k~1/n=0.0005 for center. lower sum of p_i q~O(n*(1/n)^2)=1/n=0.0005. Difference to solve p_k? For k<n, c_k includes 2p0 p_k ~5e-7 plus lower ~0.0005. def cap_square_refine(a, n, time_limit, rng):
```
   best=proj(a); best_sc=score
   avg=1/(2n-1)
   current_ratio=best_sc  # since avg relation
   # possible target lam ratios: start = min(current_ratio - 0.01, 1.7? to target 1.5)

      if no time break
      for _ in iters until time block maybe 2s:
         c,target = ... using lam=ratio*avg

            cand=proj(variant)

```

Maybe stage function:
```
def process_stage(n, inits, deadline, rng):
    best = best score among inits
    current = best.copy()
    while time + min_block < deadline:
        # deterministic cap block
        cap_deadline = min(deadline, now + cap_block)
        current, sc = cap_square_refine(current,n,cap_deadline)
        if sc<best: best=current
        # local block
        local_deadline = min(deadline, now + local_block)
        current, sc = optimize_log(current,n, local_deadline)
        if sc<best: best=current
    return best
```

Potential performance of cap recurrence in Python: Need implement dot loops efficiently.
```
def _recurrence(c, n):
    p = np.zeros(n)
    c0 = float(c[0])
    if c0 <= 1e-300:
       # find first nonzero? fallback return uniform? We'll add floor before.
    p[0] = math.sqrt(max(c0, 1e-300))
    if p[0] < 1e-12:
       # if too small, add small
    for k in range(1,n):
        if k == 1:
            lower = 0.0
        else:
            # q = p; lower = dot(p[1:k], p[k-1:0:-1])
            lower = float(np.dot(p[1:k], p[k-1:0:-1]))
        val = (float(c[k]) - lower) / (2*p[0])
        p[k] = val
    # If p contains nonfinite, fallback
    return p
```
Using p[1:k] each k. Note for k=2: p[1:2] dot p[1:0:-1]? p[1:0:-1] gives [p1], lower p1^2; coefficient c2 includes p0p2*2 + p1^2. Good.
Backward: target_rev = c[::-1], q=recurrence(target_rev,n); p=q[::-1].

Let's design O(n log n) polynomial square root for length n (coefficients 0..n-1) given C length n (target first n coeffs if forward, or reversed). We need C[0] > 0.

Newton for square root of formal power series: We want P = C^{1/2} mod z^n.
Start P = [sqrt(C0)] length m=1.
For m doubling to >=n:
Given P length m correct to m, C length 2m (or n). Compute R = inverse of P mod z^{2m}? Standard algorithm:
P_new = (P + C * inv(P)) / 2 mod z^{2m}, where inv(P) known mod z^{2m}? If only known mod m, can update inverse similarly.
Alternative simpler: Use recurrence but compute lower convolution via FFT each step? Not.
There is a direct formula using existing truncated inverse. Let's maintain inv P modulo 2m. Algorithm:
- Start length 1: P=[p0], invP=[1/p0].
- To double to length 2m (M=2m):
   We need C_M = C[:M].
   We have P_m (length m), inv_m (length m) correct mod m.
   Update inverse to length M: inv_new = inv_m * (2 - P_m * inv_m) mod z^M. Since P_m*inv_m=1 mod m, error starts at m; one Newton step gives correct mod 2m. Compute conv via FFT.
   Then P_new = 0.5 * (P_m + C_M * inv_new) mod z^M.
   Truncate P_m, inv_m = P_new[:M], inv_new[:M].
This yields P=M terms. Complexity each double: several convolutions length M, total O(n log n).

Need implement convolution of arrays with zero padding to next power 2. For formal series, no negative powers. Use `np.fft`.

```
def _series_mul(a,b,outlen):
    if outlen<=0: return np.zeros(0)
    need=len(a)+len(b)-1
    N=1
    while N<need and N<outlen: N<<=1
    # Actually for convolution truncated to outlen, we need cyclic length at least min(need,outlen)? To avoid aliasing coefficients <outlen, need N>=min(len(a),outlen)+min(len(b),outlen)-1 maybe. Use N=next_pow2(min(len(a),outlen)+min(len(b),outlen)-1).

    c=np.fft.ifft(fa*fb).real[:outlen]
    return c
```

Let's implement sqrt with maintained inverse:
```
def _poly_sqrt_series(c, n):
    c = np.asarray(c[:n], float)
    if c[0] <= 1e-300:
       # try add small? return None
    p = np.array([math.sqrt(c[0])])
    inv = np.array([1.0/p[0]])
    m=1
    while m < n:
       M = min(2*m, n)
       # Extend p and inv to length M with zeros
       pM = np.zeros(M); pM[:m]=p
       invM = np.zeros(M); invM[:m]=inv
       # Compute prod = pM * invM mod M. Since both known M zeros after m, but we can use lengths m?
       prod = _series_mul(pM, invM, M)
       tmp = np.zeros(M)
       tmp[0] = 2.0
       tmp -= prod
       inv_new = _series_mul(invM, tmp, M)
       # Compute cinv = c[:M] * inv_new mod M
       cM = c[:M]
       cinv = _series_mul(cM, inv_new, M)
       p_new = 0.5 * (pM + cinv)
       p = p_new
       inv = inv_new
       m = M
    return p
```

The standard inverse double formula can do: inv2m = inv_m * (2 - A_new_m * inv_m) mod 2m, where A_new_m is the current approximation of length 2m with known lower coefficients and zeros above m? Wait old m to new M=4: current P length m=2, but A_ext to M has first m coefficients [1,1], zeros above. To compute its inverse to M=4, we can update from old inv of A_old? The old inv at end previous step should be inverse of A_old_ext length? At previous step, after computing P length2, we should also compute inverse of this new P modulo new length 2 (or 4?) for next. The inverse of new P modulo 2 is [1,-1]. We didn't compute it; we had inv for old A_old_ext, not new. So we need after updating P, update Inv to inverse of new P modulo m (the new current m) or at least to next old length. We can compute inverse of new P modulo M directly using a function after P_new (cost). That's simpler: In each sqrt loop, after P_new computed length M, compute Inv = _series_inverse(P_new (padded to M), M) from scratch or using current? That adds cost but correct. But maybe there is a way: Newton for sqrt often does not require maintaining inverse; it uses `A = (A + B/A)/2` where division B/A is computed by convolving B with inverse of A modulo 2m; inverse of current A can be computed from previous inverse of A? Since A changes, one can compute inverse from scratch O(log M). The overall sqrt can be O(M log M) if efficient; from scratch inverse at each doubling might be O(M log^2 M), still probably fine for 8192.

Simpler: In each sqrt, for each M, compute inverse of current P (padded zeros) of length M from scratch using `_series_inverse(P_current, M)`. Then P_new=0.5*(P_current + C*inv). P_current padded to M (length m with zeros). This does inverse at M for P_current before updating. For M doubling, from scratch cost sum over powers O(n log n) with factor ~ maybe 3x. Fine.

Implement `_series_inverse(a, length)`:
```
def _series_inverse(a, length):
    a = np.asarray(a, float).copy()
    if len(a) < length: a_ext = zeros(length); a_ext[:len(a)]=a
    else: a_ext=a[:length]
    if abs(a_ext[0])<1e-300: return None
    m=1
    x = np.array([1.0/a_ext[0]])
    while m < length:
       M = min(2*m, length)
       am = a_ext[:M]
       # prod = am * x[:M]? x length m; pad to M
       prod = _series_mul(am, x, M)
       tmp = np.zeros(M); tmp[0]=2.0
       tmp -= prod
       x = _series_mul(x, tmp, M)
       m=M
    return x[:length]
```
Check for a=[1,1], length4:
m=1 x=[1]. M=2: am=[1,1]; prod=[1,1]; tmp=[1,-1]; x=[1]-? x*tmp [1,1]*[1,-1] = [1,0] length2? But inverse of 1+z mod2 should [1,-1]. Because x initial [1] (mod1). Formula x_new=x(2 - a x) with a mod M, x length m. Let's compute: a=[1,1], x=[1]; a*x=[1,1]; 2 - = [1,-1]; x* = [1]*[1,-1]=[1,-1]. I mistakenly x [1,1]. We use x length1. Good. m=2 x=[1,-1]. M=4: am=[1,1,0,0]; prod= [1,0,0,0]? (1+z)*(1-z)=1-z^2? Actually length4: [1,0,-1,0]? Let's calculate: [1,1]*[1,-1] = [1,0,-1,0]. tmp=[1,-0,1,-0]? 2 - prod = [1,0,1,0]. x*tmp: [1,-1,0,0]*[1,0,1,0] = [1,-1,1,-1]. Good inverse. Correct.
Thus from scratch inverse works.

Then sqrt:
```
def _poly_sqrt_series(c,n):
    c = np.asarray(c[:n], float)
    c0 = c[0]
    if c0 <= 1e-300: c[0]=1e-300
    m=1
    p = np.array([math.sqrt(max(c0,0))])
    while m < n:
       M = min(2*m,n)
       pM = np.zeros(M); pM[:m]=p
       inv = _series_inverse(pM, M)
       if inv is None: return None
       cM = c[:M]
       cinv = _series_mul(cM, inv, M)
       p_new = 0.5*(pM + cinv)
       p = p_new
       m=M
    return p
```
Test C=[1,2,1]. m1 p=[1]. M2 pM[1,0], inv[1,0], cM[1,2], cinv[1,2], p=[1,1]. M4? n=3 so M=3? Next pM length3 [1,1,0], inverse of 1+z mod3 [1,-1,1]. cM[1,2,1], cinv: (1+2z+z2)(1-z+z2)= [1,1,0] (coeff2:1-2+1=0). p_new [1,1,0]. Good.

```
def cap_square_one(a, ratio, n, double_sides=True):
   c=conv_self_fft(a, _next_pow2(2n-1))
   s=c.sum(); c/=s
   avg=1/(2n-1)
   lam=ratio*avg
   cmax=max
   if lam >= cmax*1.001: return None
   target=make_target(c,lam)
   variants=[]
   if double_sides or target[0]>=target[-1]:
       t_f=target.copy(); ensure endpoint 0
       p_f = sqrt_series(t_f,n) or recurrence
       add p_f, abs(p_f)
   if double_sides or target[-1]>=target[0]:
       t_b=target[::-1].copy(); ensure 0
       q=sqrt_series(t_b,n); p_b=q[::-1]
       add p_b, abs(p_b)
   best_cand=None; best_sc=score_fft(a)
   for v in variants:
       for trans in [1, -1?]: # abs already
          cand = proj(v)
          if valid:
             sc=score_fft(cand); update
             # blends
             for blend in (0.3,0.5,0.7,1.0):
                candb=(1-blend)*a + blend*cand; proj; score; update
   return best_cand,best_sc
```

```
def _soft(v):
    v=np.array(v,dtype=np.float64)
    v=np.clip(v,-40,40)
    v=v-v.max()
    e=np.exp(v)
    s=e.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite: return ones/n
    return e/s
```

`_series_mul(a,b,outlen)`:
Implement with length min len(a/outlen), etc.
```
def _series_mul(a,b,outlen):
    a=np.asarray(a,float); b=...
    la=min(len(a),outlen); lb=min(len(b),outlen)
    if la==0 or lb==0 or outlen<=0: return zeros(outlen)
    need=la+lb-1
    N=1; while N<need: N<<=1
    fa=np.fft.fft(a[:la], N); fb=np.fft.fft(b[:lb], N)
    c=np.fft.ifft(fa*fb).real[:outlen]
    return c
```
Potential aliasing: If N<need, cyclic convolution wraps coefficients >=N into lower. We set N>=need, so no wrap for full length need. Good. For outlen smaller than need, we still compute full need then truncate. This may be more work than necessary but safe. For formal series inverse, need la+lb-1 can be up to 2M-1, N next pow2 ~2M. Good.

`_poly_sqrt_series(c,n)`:
```
c=np.asarray(c, float).copy()
if len(c)<n: pad
c=c[:n]
c=np.maximum(c,0)
if c[0] < 1e-300:
    c[0]=1e-300
m=1
p=np.array([math.sqrt(c[0])])
while m<n:
   M=min(2*m,n)
   pM=np.zeros(M); pM[:m]=p
   inv=_series_inverse(pM,M)
   if inv is None or not all finite: return None
   cM=c[:M]
   cinv=_series_mul(cM,inv,M)
   p_new=0.5*(pM+cinv)
   # Clean tiny imaginary? real.
   if not all finite: return None
   p=p_new
   m=M
return p[:n]
```
Because pM padded zeros, cM length M. The series sqrt of c should satisfy p^2=c mod n. Good.

```
   if q is not None:
      p=q[::-1]
      add p, abs, max
# remove duplicates? Not necessary, but can score and take unique by L2 if many.
```
But adding p and max(p,0) same if no negative. Abs can be very different. Could include both. Maybe include `p` even with negatives? But projection will max to 0, losing if negative; abs maybe. We'll pass raw; `cap_square_one` projects variants with `proj`, which max. Also try `proj(np.abs(v))`. So _poly_sqrt_variants can just return raw p for forward/backward, and in cap_one try proj(v), proj(abs(v)). That is compressed.

Cap_one:
```
def cap_square_one(a, ratio, n, m, double=True):
   c=_conv_fft(a,m); s=c.sum(); c=c/max(s,eps); cmax=c.max()
   avg=1/(2*n-1)
   lam=ratio*avg
   if lam >= cmax*(1-1e-6): return None,cmax
   target=_make_target(c,lam)
   roots = _poly_roots(target,n,double)
   best_cand=a.copy(); best_sc=2*n*cmax  # sum1
   for p in roots:
       for cand_raw in (p, np.abs(p)):
           cand=_proj(cand_raw)
           if cand is None: continue
           sc=score_fft(cand,m)
           if sc<best_sc: update
           # blends
           for blend in (0.25,0.5,0.75):
               candb=(1-blend)*a + blend*cand
               candb=_proj(candb)
               sc=score_fft(candb,m)
               update
   if best_sc < 2*n*cmax - 1e-12: return best_cand,best_sc
   return None,None
```
But if ratio too high and no improvement, returns None. Blends with a preserve sum1 if both. Use sum normalized.

Number of roots: If double, 2. If one, 1. For each root 2 raw*4 (raw+3 blends)=8 scores. 16 per iter.

Maybe too many scores but okay. Can reduce blends to (0.5,0.8,1.0). Use `blend_values=(0.5,0.8,1.0)`; raw is blend=1.0, but raw cand is p; for abs raw separate. So for each raw, test blend 0.5 and 0.8 (since 1.0 is raw). 2 raws *3=6 per root; 12 total.

Need score_fft for each blend uses FFT and normalization; candb sum1 but due blend. Good.

`_make_target(c,lam)`:
```
L=len(c)
avg=1/L
lam=max(lam,1.005*avg)
cap=np.minimum(c,lam)
deficit=1.0 - cap.sum()
if deficit < 1e-14: target=cap
else:
   room=np.maximum(0.0, lam - cap)
   rs=room.sum()
   if deficit > 0 and rs > deficit*0.99:
       target=cap + deficit*room/rs
   else:
       target=cap + deficit/L
target=np.maximum(target,0)
# normalize (in case)
ts=target.sum()
if ts>0: target=target/ts else uniform
# endpoints tiny floor
end_floor=max(1e-15, 1e-12*max(target.max(),1.0))
if target[0] < end_floor: target[0]=end_floor
if target[-1] < end_floor: target[-1]=end_floor
target=np.maximum(target,0); target/=target.sum()
return target
```
If lam < avg? we set >=1.005avg. If current score below 1.005? impossible. Good.

```
def cap_square_refine(a,n,m,deadline,rng):
    best=_proj(a); best_sc=score_fft(best,m)
    avg=1/(2n-1)
    # ratios descending from 1.98 to 1.40 maybe

    while r>1.35:
       ratios.append(round(r,4)); r-=0.02
    # But if best_sc low, first many >score; we can pointer first <best_sc.
    ptr=0
    while ptr<len(ratios) and ratios[ptr] >= best_sc - 1e-5: ptr+=1
    last_improve=time.monotonic()
    iters_per_ratio = {n<=512:60, <=1024:40, <=2048:25, <=4096:12, else:6}
    # double orientation if n<=4096? For series sqrt cost okay but maybe; set double=True for n<=2048, else likely orientation.
    double = n <= 2048
    while time.monotonic()+3 < deadline:
       if ptr>=len(ratios):
           # generate a few lower based on best_sc if >1.40
           if best_sc > 1.42:
               ratios.append(max(1.38, best_sc - 0.02))
               # maybe sort descending? since new lower than last? If best_sc lowered, best_sc-0.02 < last maybe. append at end okay if last lower? But last might be 1.37. We need maintain descending. Simpler while ptr>=len: if best_sc>1.40: ratios.append(max(1.38, best_sc-0.02)); else break; If appended, continue (not sorted but lower than existing? If existing down to1.37 and best_sc 1.5 -> append1.48 before 1.37, violating. Better predefine enough down to 1.35.
       ratio=ratios[ptr]
       start=time.monotonic(); stall=0; improved=False
       max_iter = iters_per_ratio

             else:
                 stall+=1

             # no target reduction possible? maybe ratio >= cmax; advance?
             break
       if not improved:
           ptr += 1
       # if no improve for overall 90s, break? or increase ratio? 
       if time.monotonic()-last_improve > 120:
           # maybe break cap
           break
    return best,best_sc
```

`optimize_log` implementation more detailed:
```
def optimize_log(a_init,n,m,deadline,seed):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    v=np.log(np.maximum(a_init,1e-12))
    v = v - v.mean()
    v = np.clip(v, -25,25)
    a=_soft(v); best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score_fft(a,m)
    if not finite best: best_score=inf; a=ones/n; v=zeros
    beta1=0.9; beta2=0.999; eps=1e-8
    mu=zeros; nu=zeros; t=0
    last_improve=start; bad=0
    min_ratio=1.46
    while time.monotonic()+0.5 < deadline:
       progress = min(1,(now-start)/budget)
       # if stuck
       if time.monotonic()-last_improve > min(120,0.25*budget):
           sigma=0.1+0.3*(1-progress)
           v += rng.normal(0,sigma,n); v-=mean; clip; bad=0
           continue
       a=_soft(v)
       c=_conv_fft(a,m)
       s=sum(c); cmax=c.max(); sc=2*n*cmax/(s? if normalized sum1 csum=1; use score_fft? if a sum1 score=2n*cmax)
       if sc < best_score: best...; last_improve=now; bad=0
       else: bad +=1
       if bad > 80:
          v=best_a logs? Actually best_v stored. Store best_v when best_a update.
          v=best_v + rng.normal(0,0.05,n); center clip; bad=0; continue
       # target ratio
       delta = 0.005 + 0.06*progress
       ratio = max(min_ratio, best_score - delta)
       avg=1/(2n-1)
       lam=ratio*avg
       gap = cmax - lam
       if gap > 1e-15:
           tau = max(1e-15, 0.25*gap)
           over = np.maximum(c-lam,0)
           os=over.sum()
           if os>1e-15: over/=os
           else over = ones/L/L
           alpha_over=0.35 + 0.35*progress? maybe 0.4
       else:
           tau = max(1e-15, 0.05*cmax)
           over = np.ones(L)/L; alpha_over=0.1
       # pmax softmax
       x=(c-cmax)/tau; clip -50,0; exp; pmax=exp/sum
       p = (1-alpha_over)*pmax + alpha_over*over
       p /= p.sum()
       h=_corr(p,a,m)
       proj_coeff=dot(h,a)
       gv=a*(h-proj_coeff)
       # regularize to keep v not too extreme? subtract mean
       grms=sqrt(mean(gv*gv))
       if grms<1e-14: random continue
       gv/=grms
       t+=1; mu=...; nu=...
       mhat=mu/(1-beta1**t); vhat=...
       lr=0.25*(1-0.6*progress)   # maybe 0.2
       step=mhat/(sqrt(vhat)+eps)
       step=np.clip(step,-1,1)
       v = v - lr*step
       v -= mean; clip
    # final a score
    return best_a,best_score
```
Need store best_v: when best_score update, best_v=v.copy(). Then reset if bad.

But v update after random should maybe use best_v? If stuck, v noise plus continue. If bad, reset to best_v.

Score exact from c: Since a normalized sum may not exactly 1 due `_soft`; it does normalize, sum=1. Good.

`budget = deadline - start`. For stuck limit use max 60? We'll set `stuck_limit = max(30, min(90, 0.2*budget))`. If not improve for 60s, perturb.

Check gradient sign: We minimize sc; gradient gv as above. v -= lr step. If gradient computed for weighted objective? h = corr(p,a). gv=a*(h-dot). This is gradient of weighted sum (up to positive). Step reduces. Good.

But p over with window: h can be negative. Fine.

Adam step normalized by RMS, so lr controls v change. lr=0.3 maybe. For n large, 0.2. Use 0.35? Need tune. Without running, choose conservative 0.25. Step clipped [-1,1] -> v update max .25. Over thousands iterations enough.

Could use exact score current every iter (no extra FFT). Good.

Potential issue: Softmax p over all lags length L; if tau very small and cmax-lam small, many exp underflow; pmax maybe one hot. `over` contributes. Good.

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+max(60,budget_s-50)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    prev = get height_sequence_1
    best_a=prev normalized if possible else None; best_score=score if prev else inf
    print

    base=max(60,budget_s-50)
    for n, frac in stages:
       now=time.monotonic()
       if now+45 >= deadline: break
       stage_time=base*frac
       stage_deadline=min(deadline-10, now+stage_time)

    try evaluate_sequence? optional
```

```
def process_stage(n,cands,deadline,rng,prev_best):
    m=...
    best_a=...; best_score=...
    scored=...
    top=[a for _,a in scored[:6]]
    cap_wait=0
    start=time.monotonic()
    last_best_time=start
    while time.monotonic() + 20 < deadline:
       now=time.monotonic()
       rem=deadline-now
       # choose block sizes capped by rem
       if n<=512: cap_t=12; loc_t=25
       elif n<=1024: cap_t=20; loc_t=45
       elif n<=2048: cap_t=40; loc_t=90
       elif n<=4096: cap_t=60; loc_t=150
       else: cap_t=60; loc_t=200
       if cap_wait < 2 and rem > cap_t*0.5:
           cap_dl=min(deadline, now+min(cap_t,rem*0.4))
           a2,sc2=cap_square_refine(best_a,n,m,cap_dl,rng)
           if sc2<best_score-1e-9:
              best_a, best_score=a2,sc2; last_best_time=now; cap_wait=0
           else:
              cap_wait +=1
       # local block
       loc_t=min(loc_t, max(10, rem - (cap_wait<2? 10:0)))? Simpler loc_dl=min(deadline, now+min(loc_t,max(10,rem-15)))
       if loc_dl - time.monotonic() > 10:
           if top and rng.random()<0.2:
              start_a = top[int(rng.integers(0,len(top)))].copy()
              # add noise
              start_a = _proj(start_a + rng.normal(0,0.02/n? no, start sum1; sigma 0.02? For vector entries, use relative: start_a*(1+0.05 normal) maybe)
           else:
              start_a = best_a
           seed=int(rng.integers(0, 2**31-1))
           a2,sc2=optimize_log(start_a,n,m,loc_dl,seed)
           if sc2<best_score-1e-9:
              best_a, best_score=a2,sc2; last_best_time=time.monotonic(); cap_wait=0
              # add to top
              if len(top)<10: top.append(a2)
           if time.monotonic() - last_best_time > 180:
              # perturb best for next local? Could add random restart candidate
              pert = _proj(best_a + rng.normal(0, 0.1/n? no entries; use v noise)
              top.append(pert)
    return best_a,best_score
```

Let's write `_param_inits`:
```
def _param_inits(n, rng):
    out=[]
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    i=np.arange(n,float)
    out.append(ones(n))
    # exponentials
    for g in (-4,-2,-1,1,2,4,6):
       out.append(np.exp(g*x))
    # quadratics
    for g in (-2,2):
       for q in (-4,-2,2,4):
          out.append(np.exp(g*x+q*x*x))
    # beta endpoint
    for p in (0.2,0.5,1,2,3):
       for q in (0.2,0.5,1,2,3):
          out.append((x+0.05)**p * (1-x+0.05)**q)
    # gaussian center
    for c in (0.3,0.5,0.7):
       for w in (0.1,0.2,0.35):
          out.append(np.exp(-((x-c)/w)**2))
    # random knot positive
    for _ in range(6):
       knots=np.sort(rng.uniform(0,1,6))
       vals=np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.5,6))
       out.append(np.interp(x, knots, vals))
       # reversed
       out.append(vals[::-1] interp?) Could just reverse.
    # random Fourier
    for _ in range(4):
       f=1.0
       for k in range(1,8):
          amp=0.3/(k**0.7)
          ph=rng.uniform(0,2*pi)
          f += amp*np.cos(2*pi*k*x+ph)
          f += 0.2/(k**0.7)*np.sin(2*pi*k*x+rng.uniform(0,2*pi))
       out.append(np.maximum(f,1e-6))
    return out
```

```
import numpy as np
import time
import math

def _next_pow2(x):
    x=int(max(1,x))
    p=1
    while p<x: p <<=1
    return p
```

`_proj`:
```
def _proj(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim !=1: a=a.ravel()
    if a.size==0: return np.ones(3)
    a=a.copy()
    a[~np.isfinite(a)]=0.0
    np.maximum(a,0.0,out=a)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
       return np.full(a.size,1.0/a.size)
    a /= s
    return a
```

`_soft` as above.

`_interp`: handle n int.

`_conv_fft`:
```
def _conv_fft(a, m=None):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    if m is None: m=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
    F=np.fft.fft(a,m)
    b=np.fft.ifft(F*F).real[:2*n-1]
    b=np.maximum(b,0.0)
    return b
```
If a not normalized, c scales.

`score_fft`:
```
def score_fft(a, m=None):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.inf
    S=float(a.sum())
    if S <= 1e-300 or not finite: return inf
    c=_conv_fft(a,m)
    cmax=float(c.max())
    if not finite or cmax<=0: return inf
    return float(2.0*n*cmax/(S*S))
```

`_corr`:
```
def _corr(p,a,m):
    P=np.fft.fft(p,m); A=np.fft.fft(a,m)
    return np.fft.ifft(P*np.conj(A)).real[:a.size]
```

Series functions:
```
def _series_mul(a,b,outlen):
   a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64); b=...
   outlen=int(max(0,outlen))
   la=min(a.size,outlen); lb=min(b.size,outlen)
   if outlen==0 or la==0 or lb==0: return np.zeros(outlen)
   need=la+lb-1
   N=1
   while N<need: N <<=1
   fa=np.fft.fft(a[:la], N)
   fb=np.fft.fft(b[:lb], N)
   c=np.fft.ifft(fa*fb).real[:outlen]
   return c
```
Maybe if need large, N next pow2. OK.

`_series_inverse`:
```
def _series_inverse(a, length):
   a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
   length=int(length)
   if length<=0: return np.zeros(0)
   ext=np.zeros(length)
   m=min(a.size,length)
   ext[:m]=a[:m]
   if not np.isfinite(ext[0]) or abs(ext[0]) < 1e-300:
       return None
   x=np.array([1.0/ext[0]], dtype=np.float64)
   cur=1
   while cur < length:
      M=min(2*cur, length)
      prod=_series_mul(ext[:M], x, M)
      if not np.all(np.isfinite(prod)): return None
      tmp=np.zeros(M)
      tmp[0]=2.0
      tmp -= prod
      x=_series_mul(x, tmp, M)
      if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)): return None
      cur=M
   return x[:length]
```
Potential `ext[:M]` includes estimates beyond current known? ext has full target c? For inverse of pM, pM coefficients beyond cur unknown zero. We call with `a=pM` where pM length M with zeros beyond cur. Here ext is that pM. But `ext` includes zeros beyond cur. Good. However in `_series_inverse`, `ext[:M]` for M > len(p?)? We pass length M and pM length M. Good. But if pM has zeros beyond current, ext correct. The inverse loop uses full ext up to M (with zeros). Good.

`_poly_sqrt_series`:
```
def _poly_sqrt_series(c,n):
   c=np.asarray(c,dtype=np.float64)
   n=int(n)
   if n<=0: return np.zeros(0)
   ext=np.zeros(n)
   m=min(c.size,n); ext[:m]=c[:m]
   ext=np.maximum(ext,0.0)
   cmax=float(ext.max())
   floor=max(1e-18, 1e-15*max(cmax,1.0))
   if ext[0] < floor: ext[0]=floor
   p=np.array([math.sqrt(ext[0])], dtype=np.float64)
   cur=1
   while cur<n:
      M=min(2*cur,n)
      pM=np.zeros(M)
      pM[:cur]=p
      inv=_series_inverse(pM, M)
      if inv is None: return None
      cM=ext[:M]
      cinv=_series_mul(cM, inv, M)
      if not np.all(np.isfinite(cinv)): return None
      pnew=0.5*(pM + cinv)
      if not np.all(np.isfinite(pnew)): return None
      p=pnew
      cur=M
   return p[:n]
```
But note: In first iteration cur=1, pM length M with p[0]. _series_inverse uses full pM zeros. Good.

`_poly_roots`:
```
def _poly_roots(target,n,double=True):
   roots=[]
   L=target.size
   if double or (L>0 and target[0] >= target[-1]):
       t=target.copy()
       if t[0] < 0: t[0]=0
       p=_poly_sqrt_series(t,n)
       if p is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(p)): roots.append(p)
   if double or (L>0 and target[-1] >= target[0]):
       tb=target[::-1].copy()
       if tb[0] < 0: tb[0]=0
       q=_poly_sqrt_series(tb,n)
       if q is not None:
          p=q[::-1]
          if np.all(np.isfinite(p)): roots.append(p)
   return roots
```
If double False and both endpoints equal, first branch; okay. Maybe if first fails, try second. We can ensure at least one:
```
if not roots and not double: try other orientation manually
```

```
try_forward = double or target[0] >= target[-1]
try_backward = double or target[-1] >= target[0]
if not try_forward and not try_backward: try_forward=True
```

`_make_target`:
```
def _make_target(c,lam):
   c=np.asarray(c,float).copy()
   L=c.size
   cs=c.sum()
   if cs>0: c/=cs
   else: c=np.ones(L)/L
   avg=1.0/L
   if lam < 1.01*avg: lam=1.01*avg
   cap=np.minimum(c,lam)
   deficit=1.0 - float(cap.sum())
   if deficit > 1e-14:
      room=np.maximum(0.0, lam - cap)
      rs=float(room.sum())
      if rs > deficit*0.99:
          target=cap + deficit*room/rs
      else:
          target=cap + deficit/L
   else:
      target=cap.copy()
   target=np.maximum(target,0.0)
   ts=float(target.sum())
   if ts <=0: target=np.ones(L)/L
   else: target/=ts
   end_floor=max(1e-18, 1e-14*max(float(target.max()),1.0))
   if target[0] < end_floor: target[0]=end_floor
   if target[-1] < end_floor: target[-1]=end_floor
   target=np.maximum(target,0.0)
   ts=float(target.sum())
   if ts>0: target/=ts
   return target
```
Note if lam much lower than avg? we set >1.01 avg so room sum enough. Good.

`cap_square_one`:
```
def cap_square_one(a, ratio, n, m, double=True):
    a=_proj(a)
    c=_conv_fft(a,m)
    cs=c.sum()
    if cs>0: c=c/cs
    cmax=float(c.max())
    sc_current=2*n*cmax  # if sum1, but a sum1.
    avg=1/(2*n-1)
    lam=ratio*avg
    if lam >= cmax*(1-1e-7):
       return None
    target=_make_target(c,lam)
    roots=_poly_roots(target,n,double)
    best_cand=a
    best_sc=sc_current
    blends=(0.35,0.65)
    for p in roots:
       for raw in (p, np.abs(p)):
           cand=_proj(raw)
           options=[cand]
           for bl in blends:
               options.append(_proj((1-bl)*a + bl*cand))
           for cand_o in options:
               sc=score_fft(cand_o,m)
               if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                   best_sc=sc; best_cand=cand_o
    if best_sc < sc_current - 1e-12:
       return (best_cand,best_sc)
    return None
```
Score_fft recomputes c for each cand; maybe can optimize using normalized? ok.
Potential many score; if score_fft is slower, still okay.

Could use precomputed m for cand size same. Good.

`cap_square_refine`:

```
def cap_square_refine(a,n,m,deadline,rng):
   best=_proj(a); best_sc=score_fft(best,m)
   if not finite: return ones,n
   double = n <= 2048
   # ratios
   ratios=[]
   r=1.99
   while r>1.34:
      ratios.append(round(r,4)); r-=0.02
   ptr=0
   while ptr<len(ratios) and ratios[ptr] >= best_sc - 1e-5:
      ptr+=1
   if ptr>=len(ratios):
      if best_sc > 1.38:
         ratios.append(max(1.32, best_sc - 0.02))
         ptr=len(ratios)-1
      else:
         return best,best_sc
   if n <=512: iters=30; block=2.5
   elif n<=1024: iters=20; block=3.0
   elif n<=2048: iters=12; block=3.5
   else: iters=6; block=3.0
   last_improve=time.monotonic()
   while time.monotonic()+2 < deadline:
       # if ptr too far
       while ptr < len(ratios) and ratios[ptr] >= best_sc - 1e-5:
           ptr +=1
       if ptr>=len(ratios):
           if best_sc > 1.40:
              ratios.append(max(1.32, best_sc - 0.02))
              ptr=len(ratios)-1
              # But if appended, it should be lower than previous? Could be higher if best_sc improved? Since best_sc only decreases, best_sc-0.02 <= previous best_sc-0.02, likely lower. OK.
           else: break
       ratio=ratios[ptr]
       start_block=time.monotonic()
       stall=0
       improved_block=False
       while stall < iters and time.monotonic()-start_block < block:
           res=cap_square_one(best, ratio, n, m, double)
           if res is not None:
               cand,sc=res
               if sc < best_sc - 1e-10:
                   best=cand; best_sc=sc; improved_block=True; stall=0; last_improve=time.monotonic()
               else:
                   stall +=1
           else:
               break
       if not improved_block:
           ptr += 1
       if time.monotonic()-last_improve > 120:
           # stop cap after long no improve
           break
   return best,best_sc
```

```
def optimize_log(a_init,n,m,deadline,seed):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    start=time.monotonic()
    budget=max(1.0, deadline-start)
    a=_proj(a_init)
    v=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-12))
    v=v-float(v.mean()); v=np.clip(v,-25,25)
    a_s=_soft(v); best_a=a_s.copy(); best_v=v.copy(); best_score=score_fft(a_s,m)
    if not finite: best_score=inf; best_a=ones; best_v=zeros
    beta1=0.9; beta2=0.999; eps=1e-8
    mu=np.zeros(n); nu=np.zeros(n); t=0
    last_improve=start; bad=0
    min_ratio=1.46
    while time.monotonic()+0.5 < deadline:
       progress=min(1.0, (time.monotonic()-start)/budget)
       if time.monotonic()-last_improve > max(20,min(80,0.2*budget)):
           sigma=0.08+0.25*(1-progress)
           v=best_v + rng.normal(0.0, sigma*n? no, v dimension log probs; sigma 0.2) # Use sigma 0.15
           v -= v.mean(); v=np.clip(v,-25,25); bad=0
           continue
       v=np.clip(v,-25,25); v -= v.mean()
       a=_soft(v)
       c=_conv_fft(a,m)
       # Since a normalized, csum ~1; but due FFT may not exactly. Score:
       cmax=float(c.max())
       csum=float(c.sum())
       if csum>0:
          sc=2.0*n*cmax/(csum) # because score=2n cmax/S_a^2; csum = S_a^2 (if c from a). For a sum1, csum=1. Use csum robust.
       else: sc=inf
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
           best_score=sc; best_a=a.copy(); best_v=v.copy(); last_improve=time.monotonic(); bad=0
       else:
           bad +=1
       if not np.isfinite(sc):
           v=best_v; continue
       if bad > 60:
           v=best_v + rng.normal(0,0.08,n)
           v -= v.mean(); v=np.clip(v,-25,25); bad=0
           continue
       # target ratio
       delta=0.004 + 0.05*progress
       ratio=max(min_ratio, best_score - delta)
       avg=1.0/(2*n-1)
       lam=ratio*avg
       gap=cmax - lam
       L=c.size
       if gap > 1e-15:
          tau=max(1e-15, 0.2*gap)
          over=np.maximum(c-lam,0.0)
          os=float(over.sum())
          if os > 1e-15: over /= os
          else: over=np.ones(L)/L
          alpha=0.35 + 0.30*progress
       else:
          tau=max(1e-15, 0.05*cmax)
          over=np.ones(L)/L
          alpha=0.15
       x=(c-cmax)/tau
       x=np.clip(x,-50.0,0.0)
       e=np.exp(x)
       es=float(e.sum())
       if es <=0 or not finite: pmax=np.ones(L)/L
       else: pmax=e/es
       p=(1-alpha)*pmax + alpha*over
       ps=float(p.sum())
       if ps>0: p/=ps
       h=_corr(p,a,m)
       if not all finite: random continue
       proj=float(np.dot(h,a))
       gv=a*(h-proj)
       grms=float(np.sqrt(np.mean(gv*gv)))
       if grms < 1e-14:
           v += rng.normal(0,0.05,n); v-=mean; clip; continue
       gv/=grms
       t +=1
       mu=beta1*mu +(1-beta1)*gv
       nu=beta2*nu +(1-beta2)*(gv*gv)
       b1=beta1**t; b2=beta2**t
       mhat=mu/max(1e-12,1-b1)
       vhat=nu/max(1e-12,1-b2)
       lr=0.22*(1 - 0.5*progress)
       step=mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+eps)
       step=np.clip(step,-1.0,1.0)
       v = v - lr*step
       v -= v.mean(); v=np.clip(v,-25,25)
    # final score
    a=_soft(v); sc=score_fft(a,m)
    if finite and sc<best_score: update
    return best_a,best_score
```

```
def _select_candidates(cands,m,nr,maxkeep=8,dist=0.045):
    scored=[]; seen=[]
    for a in cands:
       a=_proj(a)
       if a.size != nr: a=_interp(a,nr)
       sc=score_fft(a,m)
       if not finite: continue
       diff=True
       for b in seen:

          if d < dist: diff=False; break
       if diff:
          scored.append((sc,a)); seen.append(a)
```

```
              v-=v.mean(); pert=_soft(np.clip(v,-25,25))
              if len(top)<12: top.append(pert)
   return _proj(best_a), float(best_score)
```

```python
"""
Search for non-negative sequence heights minimizing
    2 * n * max(convolve(a, a)) / sum(a)^2.

The code combines:
  - a deterministic "cap-and-square-root" map: cap the current convolution
    at a target maximum, rebalance mass, then take the algebraic square root
    of the resulting polynomial (series Newton in Fourier space);
  - Adam-based smoothed subgradient refinement in log-softmax space;
  - multi-start constructive shapes and progressive length scaling.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math


def _next_pow2(x):
    x = int(max(1, x))
    p = 1
    while p < x:
        p <<= 1
    return p


def _proj(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1:
        a = a.ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(3, dtype=np.float64)
    a = a.copy()
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    np.maximum(a, 0.0, out=a)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(a.size, 1.0 / a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    a /= s
    return a


def _soft(v):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.clip(v, -30.0, 30.0)
    v = v - float(v.max())
    e = np.exp(v)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(v.shape, 1.0 / max(1, v.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return e / s


def _interp(a, n):
    n = int(max(1, n))
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1:
        a = a.ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a = a / s
    if a.size == n:
        return a.copy()
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, a.size)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    out = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    ss = float(out.sum())
    if ss <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(ss):
        out.fill(1.0 / n)
    else:
        out /= ss
    return out


def _conv_fft(a, m=None):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if m is None:
        m = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    F = np.fft.fft(a, m)
    b = np.fft.ifft(F * F).real[:2 * n - 1]
    b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    return b


def score_fft(a, m=None):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(S):
        return np.inf
    c = _conv_fft(a, m)
    cmax = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(cmax) or cmax <= 0.0:
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * cmax / (S * S))


def _corr(p, a, m):
    P = np.fft.fft(p, m)
    A = np.fft.fft(a, m)
    return np.fft.ifft(P * np.conj(A)).real[:a.size]


def _pair_item(x):
    return x[0]


def _series_mul(a, b, outlen):
    outlen = int(max(0, outlen))
    if outlen == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    la = min(a.size, outlen)
    lb = min(b.size, outlen)
    if la == 0 or lb == 0:
        return np.zeros(outlen, dtype=np.float64)
    need = la + lb - 1
    N = 1
    while N < need:
        N <<= 1
    fa = np.fft.fft(a[:la], N)
    fb = np.fft.fft(b[:lb], N)
    c = np.fft.ifft(fa * fb).real[:outlen]
    return c


def _series_inverse(a, length):
    length = int(max(1, length))
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    ext = np.zeros(length, dtype=np.float64)
    m = min(a.size, length)
    ext[:m] = a[:m]
    if not np.isfinite(ext[0]) or abs(ext[0]) < 1e-300:
        return None
    x = np.array([1.0 / ext[0]], dtype=np.float64)
    cur = 1
    while cur < length:
        M = min(2 * cur, length)
        prod = _series_mul(ext[:M], x, M)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(prod)):
            return None
        tmp = np.zeros(M, dtype=np.float64)
        tmp[0] = 2.0
        tmp -= prod
        x = _series_mul(x, tmp, M)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
            return None
        cur = M
    return x[:length]


def _poly_sqrt_series(c, n):
    n = int(max(1, n))
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    ext = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    m = min(c.size, n)
    ext[:m] = c[:m]
    ext = np.maximum(ext, 0.0)
    cmax = float(ext.max())
    floor = max(1e-15, 1e-9 * max(cmax, 1.0))
    if ext[0] < floor:
        ext[0] = floor
    p = np.array([math.sqrt(max(ext[0], 0.0))], dtype=np.float64)
    cur = 1
    while cur < n:
        M = min(2 * cur, n)
        pM = np.zeros(M, dtype=np.float64)
        pM[:cur] = p
        inv = _series_inverse(pM, M)
        if inv is None:
            return None
        cM = ext[:M]
        cinv = _series_mul(cM, inv, M)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(cinv)):
            return None
        pnew = 0.5 * (pM + cinv)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(pnew)):
            return None
        p = pnew
        cur = M
    return p[:n]


def _poly_roots(target, n, double=True):
    roots = []
    target = np.asarray(target, dtype=np.float64)
    if target.size == 0:
        return roots
    try_forward = double or (target[0] >= target[-1])
    try_backward = double or (target[-1] >= target[0])
    if not try_forward and not try_backward:
        try_forward = True
    if try_forward:
        t = target.copy()
        if t[0] < 0.0:
            t[0] = 0.0
        p = _poly_sqrt_series(t, n)
        if p is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
            roots.append(p)
    if try_backward:
        tb = target[::-1].copy()
        if tb[0] < 0.0:
            tb[0] = 0.0
        q = _poly_sqrt_series(tb, n)
        if q is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(q)):
            p = q[::-1]
            roots.append(p)
    return roots


def _make_target(c, lam):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    L = int(c.size)
    if L == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    cs = float(c.sum())
    if cs > 0.0 and np.isfinite(cs):
        c /= cs
    else:
        c = np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / float(L)
    avg = 1.0 / L
    if lam < 1.01 * avg:
        lam = 1.01 * avg
    cap = np.minimum(c, lam)
    deficit = 1.0 - float(cap.sum())
    if deficit > 1e-14:
        room = np.maximum(0.0, lam - cap)
        rs = float(room.sum())
        if rs > deficit * 0.99:
            target = cap + deficit * room / rs
        else:
            target = cap + deficit / L
    else:
        target = cap.copy()
    target = np.maximum(target, 0.0)
    ts = float(target.sum())
    if ts <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(ts):
        target = np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / float(L)
    else:
        target /= ts
    end_floor = max(1e-15, 1e-12 * max(float(target.max()), 1.0))
    if target[0] < end_floor:
        target[0] = end_floor
    if target[-1] < end_floor:
        target[-1] = end_floor
    target = np.maximum(target, 0.0)
    ts = float(target.sum())
    if ts > 1e-300 and np.isfinite(ts):
        target /= ts
    return target


def cap_square_one(a, ratio, n, m, double=True):
    a = _proj(a)
    c = _conv_fft(a, m)
    cs = float(c.sum())
    if cs > 0.0 and np.isfinite(cs):
        c = c / cs
    cmax = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(cmax) or cmax <= 0.0:
        return None
    sc_current = float(2.0 * n * cmax)
    avg = 1.0 / (2 * n - 1)
    lam = float(ratio) * avg
    if lam >= cmax * (1.0 - 1e-7):
        return None
    target = _make_target(c, lam)
    roots = _poly_roots(target, n, double)
    best_cand = a
    best_sc = sc_current
    blends = (0.35, 0.65)
    for p in roots:
        for raw in (p, np.abs(p)):
            cand = _proj(raw)
            candidates = [cand]
            for bl in blends:
                candidates.append(_proj((1.0 - bl) * a + bl * cand))
            for cand_o in candidates:
                sc = score_fft(cand_o, m)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-11:
                    best_sc = sc
                    best_cand = cand_o
    if best_sc < sc_current - 1e-11:
        return best_cand, float(best_sc)
    return None


def cap_square_refine(a, n, m, deadline, rng):
    n = int(n)
    best = _proj(a)
    best_sc = score_fft(best, m)
    if not np.isfinite(best_sc):
        best = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        best_sc = 2.0
    double = n <= 2048
    ratios = []
    r = 1.99
    while r > 1.34:
        ratios.append(round(r, 4))
        r -= 0.02
    ptr = 0
    while ptr < len(ratios) and ratios[ptr] >= best_sc - 1e-5:
        ptr += 1
    if ptr >= len(ratios):
        if best_sc > 1.38:
            ratios.append(max(1.32, best_sc - 0.02))
            ptr = len(ratios) - 1
        else:
            return best, float(best_sc)
    if n <= 512:
        iters = 35
        block = 2.5
    elif n <= 1024:
        iters = 22
        block = 3.0
    elif n <= 2048:
        iters = 14
        block = 3.5
    else:
        iters = 7
        block = 3.0
    last_improve = time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic() + 2.0 < deadline:
        while ptr < len(ratios) and ratios[ptr] >= best_sc - 1e-5:
            ptr += 1
        if ptr >= len(ratios):
            if best_sc > 1.40:
                ratios.append(max(1.32, best_sc - 0.02))
                ptr = len(ratios) - 1
            else:
                break
        ratio = ratios[ptr]
        start_block = time.monotonic()
        stall = 0
        improved_block = False
        while stall < iters and time.monotonic() - start_block < block:
            res = cap_square_one(best, ratio, n, m, double)
            if res is not None:
                cand, sc = res
                if sc < best_sc - 1e-10:
                    best = cand
                    best_sc = sc
                    improved_block = True
                    stall = 0
                    last_improve = time.monotonic()
                else:
                    stall += 1
            else:
                break
        if not improved_block:
            ptr += 1
        if time.monotonic() - last_improve > 120.0:
            break
    return best, float(best_sc)


def optimize_log(a_init, n, m, deadline, seed):
    n = int(n)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    start = time.monotonic()
    budget = max(1.0, deadline - start)
    a = _proj(a_init)
    v = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-12))
    v = v - float(v.mean())
    v = np.clip(v, -30.0, 30.0)
    a_s = _soft(v)
    best_a = a_s.copy()
    best_v = v.copy()
    best_score = score_fft(a_s, m)
    if not np.isfinite(best_score):
        best_a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        best_v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        best_score = 2.0
    beta1 = 0.9
    beta2 = 0.999
    eps = 1e-8
    mu = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    nu = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    t = 0
    last_improve = start
    bad = 0
    min_ratio = 1.46
    while time.monotonic() + 0.5 < deadline:
        progress = min(1.0, (time.monotonic() - start) / budget)
        stuck_limit = max(25.0, min(85.0, 0.22 * budget))
        if time.monotonic() - last_improve > stuck_limit:
            sigma = 0.08 + 0.25 * (1.0 - progress)
            v = best_v + rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n)
            v = v - float(v.mean())
            v = np.clip(v, -30.0, 30.0)
            bad = 0
            continue
        v = v - float(v.mean())
        v = np.clip(v, -30.0, 30.0)
        a = _soft(v)
        c = _conv_fft(a, m)
        csum = float(c.sum())
        cmax = float(c.max())
        if csum > 1e-300 and np.isfinite(cmax):
            sc = float(2.0 * n * cmax / csum)
        else:
            sc = np.inf
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = sc
            best_a = a.copy()
            best_v = v.copy()
            last_improve = time.monotonic()
            bad = 0
        else:
            bad += 1
        if not np.isfinite(sc):
            v = best_v.copy()
            continue
        if bad > 70:
            v = best_v + rng.normal(0.0, 0.08, n)
            v = v - float(v.mean())
            v = np.clip(v, -30.0, 30.0)
            bad = 0
            continue
        delta = 0.004 + 0.055 * progress
        ratio = max(min_ratio, best_score - delta)
        avg = 1.0 / (2 * n - 1)
        lam = float(ratio) * avg
        gap = cmax - lam
        L = int(c.size)
        if gap > 1e-15:
            tau = max(1e-15, 0.20 * gap)
            over = np.maximum(c - lam, 0.0)
            os = float(over.sum())
            if os > 1e-15:
                over /= os
            else:
                over = np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / float(L)
            alpha = 0.35 + 0.30 * progress
        else:
            tau = max(1e-15, 0.05 * cmax)
            over = np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / float(L)
            alpha = 0.15
        x = (c - cmax) / tau
        x = np.clip(x, -50.0, 0.0)
        e = np.exp(x)
        es = float(e.sum())
        if es <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(es):
            pmax = np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / float(L)
        else:
            pmax = e / es
        p = (1.0 - alpha) * pmax + alpha * over
        ps = float(p.sum())
        if ps <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(ps):
            p = np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / float(L)
        else:
            p /= ps
        h = _corr(p, a, m)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(h)):
            v = v + rng.normal(0.0, 0.05, n)
            v = v - float(v.mean())
            v = np.clip(v, -30.0, 30.0)
            continue
        proj_coeff = float(np.dot(h, a))
        gv = a * (h - proj_coeff)
        grms = float(np.sqrt(np.mean(gv * gv)))
        if grms < 1e-14 or not np.isfinite(grms):
            v = v + rng.normal(0.0, 0.05, n)
            v = v - float(v.mean())
            v = np.clip(v, -30.0, 30.0)
            continue
        gv = gv / grms
        t += 1
        mu = beta1 * mu + (1.0 - beta1) * gv
        nu = beta2 * nu + (1.0 - beta2) * (gv * gv)
        b1 = beta1 ** t
        b2 = beta2 ** t
        denom1 = max(1e-12, 1.0 - b1)
        denom2 = max(1e-12, 1.0 - b2)
        mhat = mu / denom1
        vhat = nu / denom2
        lr = 0.30 * (1.0 - 0.60 * progress)
        step = mhat / (np.sqrt(vhat) + eps)
        step = np.clip(step, -0.80, 0.80)
        v = v - lr * step
        v = v - float(v.mean())
        v = np.clip(v, -30.0, 30.0)
    a = _soft(v)
    sc = score_fft(a, m)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
        best_score = sc
        best_a = a
    return best_a, float(best_score)


def _param_inits(n, rng):
    n = int(max(8, n))
    out = []
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    out.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))
    for g in (-4.0, -2.0, -1.0, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0, 6.0):
        out.append(np.exp(g * x))
    for g in (-2.0, 2.0):
        for q in (-4.0, -2.0, 2.0, 4.0):
            out.append(np.exp(g * x + q * x * x))
    for p in (0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 3.0):
        for q in (0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 3.0):
            out.append((x + 0.05) ** p * (1.0 - x + 0.05) ** q)
    for ctr in (0.25, 0.4, 0.5, 0.6, 0.75):
        for w in (0.08, 0.18, 0.30, 0.45):
            out.append(np.exp(-((x - ctr) / w) ** 2))
    for _ in range(8):
        knots = np.sort(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0, 7))
        vals = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.55, 7))
        out.append(np.interp(x, knots, vals))
        out.append(np.interp(x, knots[::-1], vals[::-1]))
    for _ in range(5):
        f = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for k in range(1, 9):
            amp = 0.32 / (float(k) ** 0.75)
            ph = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
            f += amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * k * x + ph)
            amp2 = 0.20 / (float(k) ** 0.85)
            ph2 = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
            f += amp2 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * k * x + ph2)
        out.append(np.maximum(f, 1e-6))
    return out


def make_initial_candidates(n, prev_best, rng):
    n = int(max(8, n))
    cands = []
    if prev_best is not None:
        cands.append(_interp(prev_best, n))
        base = _interp(prev_best, n)
        for sigma in (0.08, 0.16):
            vv = np.log(np.maximum(base, 1e-12)) + rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n)
            vv = vv - float(vv.mean())
            cands.append(_soft(np.clip(vv, -30.0, 30.0)))
    cands.extend(_param_inits(n, rng))
    return cands


def _select_candidates(cands, m, n, maxkeep=8, dist=0.045):
    n = int(n)
    scored = []
    seen = []
    for a in cands:
        a = _proj(a)
        if a.size != n:
            a = _interp(a, n)
        sc = score_fft(a, m)
        if not np.isfinite(sc):
            continue
        diverse = True
        for b in seen:
            d = float(np.sqrt(np.sum((a - b) * (a - b))))
            if d < dist:
                diverse = False
                break
        if diverse:
            scored.append((sc, a))
            seen.append(a)
            if len(seen) >= 45 and len(scored) >= maxkeep:
                break
    scored.sort(key=_pair_item)
    return scored[:maxkeep]


def process_stage(n, cands, deadline, rng, prev_best, prev_score):
    n = int(max(8, n))
    m = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    if prev_best is not None:
        best_a = _proj(prev_best)
        if best_a.size != n:
            best_a = _interp(best_a, n)
        best_score = score_fft(best_a, m)
        if not np.isfinite(best_score):
            best_a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
            best_score = 2.0
    else:
        best_a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        best_score = 2.0
    if prev_score is not None and np.isfinite(prev_score) and n == prev_best.size:
        best_score = min(best_score, float(prev_score))
    cands = list(cands)
    cands.append(best_a.copy())
    scored = _select_candidates(cands, m, n, maxkeep=8, dist=0.05)
    if scored:
        sc0, a0 = scored[0]
        if sc0 < best_score:
            best_score = sc0
            best_a = a0
    top = [a for _, a in scored[:6]]
    if not top:
        top = [best_a.copy()]
    cap_wait = 0
    last_best = time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic() + 30.0 < deadline:
        now = time.monotonic()
        rem = max(0.0, deadline - now)
        if n <= 512:
            cap_t = 12.0
            loc_t = 28.0
        elif n <= 1024:
            cap_t = 22.0
            loc_t = 55.0
        elif n <= 2048:
            cap_t = 42.0
            loc_t = 95.0
        elif n <= 4096:
            cap_t = 60.0
            loc_t = 140.0
        else:
            cap_t = 45.0
            loc_t = 210.0
        if cap_wait < 2 and rem > max(20.0, cap_t * 0.5):
            cap_dl = min(deadline, now + min(cap_t, max(6.0, rem * 0.35)))
            if cap_dl - time.monotonic() > 6.0:
                a2, sc2 = cap_square_refine(best_a, n, m, cap_dl, rng)
                if a2 is not None and np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_score - 1e-9:
                    best_a = _proj(a2)
                    best_score = float(sc2)
                    last_best = time.monotonic()
                    cap_wait = 0
                else:
                    cap_wait += 1
        now = time.monotonic()
        rem = max(0.0, deadline - now)
        loc_t = min(loc_t, max(12.0, rem - 12.0))
        if loc_t > 12.0:
            loc_dl = min(deadline, now + loc_t)
            if top and rng.random() < 0.25:
                idx = int(rng.integers(0, len(top)))
                start_a = _proj(top[idx].copy())
                if rng.random() < 0.5:
                    vv = np.log(np.maximum(start_a, 1e-12)) + rng.normal(0.0, 0.08, n)
                    vv = vv - float(vv.mean())
                    start_a = _soft(np.clip(vv, -30.0, 30.0))
            else:
                start_a = best_a.copy()
            seed = int(rng.integers(0, 2 ** 31 - 1))
            a2, sc2 = optimize_log(start_a, n, m, loc_dl, seed)
            if a2 is not None and np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_score - 1e-9:
                best_a = _proj(a2)
                best_score = float(sc2)
                last_best = time.monotonic()
                cap_wait = 0
                if len(top) < 10:
                    top.append(best_a.copy())
            if time.monotonic() - last_best > 180.0:
                vv = np.log(np.maximum(best_a, 1e-12)) + rng.normal(0.0, 0.18, n)
                vv = vv - float(vv.mean())
                pert = _soft(np.clip(vv, -30.0, 30.0))
                if len(top) < 12:
                    top.append(pert)
    return _proj(best_a), float(best_score)


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """Entry point: returns a list of non-negative floats."""
    try:
        seed = int(seed)
    except Exception:
        seed = 42
    try:
        budget_s = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget_s = 2400.0
    budget_s = max(120.0, budget_s)
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(60.0, budget_s - 60.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    prev = None
    try:
        cand = globals().get("height_sequence_1")
        if cand is not None:
            arr = np.asarray(cand, dtype=np.float64)
            if arr.ndim != 1:
                arr = arr.ravel()
            if arr.size > 0 and np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
                arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
                ss = float(arr.sum())
                if ss > 0.0:
                    prev = arr / ss
    except Exception:
        prev = None
    if prev is not None:
        best_a = _proj(prev)
        best_score = score_fft(best_a)
    else:
        best_a = np.ones(512, dtype=np.float64) / 512.0
        best_score = 2.0
    print("Initial best: n=%d score=%.9f" % (len(best_a), best_score), flush=True)
    backup_2048 = None
    backup_2048_score = np.inf
    base = max(60.0, budget_s - 60.0)
    stages = [
        (512, 0.025),
        (1024, 0.065),
        (2048, 0.40),
        (4096, 0.44),
        (8192, 0.07),
    ]
    for st_n, frac in stages:
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now + 45.0 >= deadline:
            break
        stage_time = max(30.0, base * frac)
        stage_deadline = min(deadline - 6.0, now + stage_time)
        if stage_deadline <= time.monotonic() + 20.0:
            break
        print("Stage n=%d budget=%.1fs current_best=%.9f" % (st_n, stage_deadline - now, best_score), flush=True)
        cands = make_initial_candidates(st_n, best_a, rng)
        a_new, sc_new = process_stage(st_n, cands, stage_deadline, rng, best_a, best_score)
        if a_new is not None and np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < best_score - 1e-9:
            best_a = _proj(a_new)
            best_score = float(sc_new)
        if st_n == 2048 and best_a is not None and np.isfinite(best_score):
            backup_2048 = _interp(best_a, 2048) if best_a.size != 2048 else _proj(best_a)
            backup_2048_score = score_fft(backup_2048, _next_pow2(2 * 2048 - 1))
        print("After n=%d: best=%.9f length=%d" % (st_n, best_score, len(best_a)), flush=True)
    now = time.monotonic()
    rem = max(0.0, deadline - now)
    if best_a is not None and rem > 80.0:
        n = int(best_a.size)
        m = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
        if n <= 4096:
            cap_dl = min(deadline, now + min(70.0, rem * 0.30))
            if cap_dl - time.monotonic() > 10.0:
                a2, sc2 = cap_square_refine(best_a, n, m, cap_dl, rng)
                if a2 is not None and np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_score - 1e-9:
                    best_a = _proj(a2)
                    best_score = float(sc2)
        now = time.monotonic()
        rem = max(0.0, deadline - now)
        if rem > 35.0:
            loc_dl = min(deadline, now + rem * 0.85)
            seed2 = int(rng.integers(0, 2 ** 31 - 1))
            a2, sc2 = optimize_log(best_a, n, m, loc_dl, seed2)
            if a2 is not None and np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_score - 1e-9:
                best_a = _proj(a2)
                best_score = float(sc2)
    if backup_2048 is not None and best_a.size != 2048:
        if backup_2048_score < best_score - 1e-4:
            best_a = backup_2048
            best_score = backup_2048_score
    now = time.monotonic()
    rem = max(0.0, deadline - now)
    if best_a is not None and best_a.size <= 2048 and rem > 8.0:
        eval_fn = globals().get("evaluate_sequence")
        if callable(eval_fn):
            try:
                exact = float(eval_fn([float(x) for x in best_a]))
                print("Final FFT=%.9f exact=%.9f n=%d" % (best_score, exact, len(best_a)), flush=True)
                if not np.isfinite(exact):
                    raise ValueError("bad exact")
                if exact < best_score + 1e-3:
                    best_score = exact
            except Exception:
                pass
    if best_a is None:
        best_a = np.ones(512, dtype=np.float64) / 512.0
    best_a = _proj(best_a)
    return [float(x) for x in best_a]
```
